Existing native `ScopeRecovery.ProcessCreation()` supplies a generation-bound Windows process creation time, but the durable JS record uses `{pid, bootId, startTicks}`. Substituting the native generation without a versioned semantic contract would reinterpret persistent records; no fake boot ID, PID-only identity or alternate subsystem is permitted. This reaches the decision's process-identity/durable-format STOP boundary before implementation.

Whether that policy may be extended to workflow evidence requires Φ Main's explicit security boundary decision. ## Continuation — native process ownership candidate

Resumed from `d8a7834b861a231f450b53beb1d8a476eb4f7eac`, exact branch and existing native CI confirmed; protected/auth hashes unchanged. No foundation redesign or authentication/routing change.

Implemented a Windows x64 system-.NET helper from tracked C# source and a shared-interface dispatch for trusted non-interactive verification only. Native Job assignment occurs atomically through `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST` at process creation, before resume; only three explicit stdio handles are inherited, never the Job handle. Job limits are code-owned (32 processes, no breakaway); lifetime <=120s. Normal exit, timeout and failure terminate the owned Job; caller death waits on a generation-checked parent handle; abrupt helper death uses kill-on-last-close. Requests use a bounded stdin pipe, not request files or command-line payloads. Node bounds output/input and preserves failed exit/abort semantics. Build identity is local integrity evidence, NOT an ACL/signed-runtime attestation or sandbox certificate.

Original five subsequent cases failed startup recovery before fixture creation: quarantine cascade, not five independent ownership defects. Historical Win32-5 logs lack the original NTSTATUS/holder, so no retrospective AV/PID attribution is claimed. Exact investigation, falsified controls, rights/identities and limits: [technical report](phi-native-cleanup-regression.md).

Acquisition now retries only the two proven NTSTATUS values while its parent stays pinned, before the first successful child open. Root identity still must match. Disposition 145 retries only the same ordinary-directory handle; no rescan of new data, path reopen, ACL/readonly changes or unknown-resource deletion. Both phases share the original 5-second monotonic budget; no per-entry reset. Permanently blocked objects still FAIL and retain the journal; new-scope startup remains fail-closed. Explicit held/released root/file/child-directory controls and durable quarantine/recovery/idempotence pass. Primary safe receipts now survive a second final-recovery failure; nested clone redaction retains exact native operation metadata.
